# Seed 74 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - the after-seed-73 batch's first run: the true front reported, one round first, adaptive operators (Iteration 549)

`seed 74 10h widths noevolved then experiment review-checks.py spread then experiment appbench.py --zip
~/Downloads/appbench.zip --sample 16 then experiment clockcheck.py --sample 8` at 8eda91e (Iteration 544). The chain
came back "failed": its three experiments ran and wrote their reports, then each failed at its packing - a regression
of Iteration 541, below; the seed's own pack is whole. 33,902 designs, generations 0-132: 21,507 alive, 8,470 stopped
after one round, 3,887 screened, 37 dead of "wrong output: apps" (every one cpt16) and 1 on the corpus; 3,390 designs an
hour (seed 73: 2,989; 72: 2,916). Speed over the seven workloads, as since seed 71.

**The front reported is the true one** (the batch's first_front): 238 designs, the same 238 the records give
(results/drafts/seed74-front-549.py); the old staircase on the same records keeps 144 - prediction 527 holds (1.65
times).

**The fixed-box hypervolume: 0.35667** - the stopping series' first run on the method (seed 73's 0.34915 was on the old
one: +2.2%, for scale, not for the count). The extremes: fastest 3cf671fa08 0.1402, smallest b304ba5dfc 14,079 bytes,
least memory 112 KB, fastest at 32 bits 2aae600c18 0.1740 (seed 73's: 0.1399, 14,055, 112, 0.1772).

**One round first - prediction 529 holds**: 8,470 children stopped after one round, 28.4% of those measured (a quarter
to two fifths); 885 of them audited in full, none on the front; 3,390 designs an hour, 16.3% above seed 72's.

**Adaptive operators - prediction 526 fails**: crossover made 27.8% of the children (the last fifth 23.6%; seed 72:
36.6%), borrowing 40.5%, mutation 31.7%; the front's entries per child 0.705% (238 of 33,760), against seed 72's 0.71%
- no gain.

**jitos - prediction 534 fails**: on none of the 238. The JIT designs: 50 of the front and all 23 of its fastest tenth;
jitmul on 25, jitfuse 5, jitexec 1 (seed 73: 45, 7, 6).

**The votes** (the true front): all four - jitdoes, jitcfa, jitrp, jitos, knumber, jitret, jitvar; over 72-74
**jitrp is a freeze candidate** (12 of 12, no keep vote) - a freeze changes the method and restarts the stopping count:
the owner's to decide. jitret 11 of 12 with a keep vote: kept.

**The clock test** (without cycles: kernel.perf_event_paranoid 4): the fastest eight's time against the run 1.079 alone,
1.039 beside benchmarks, 1.056 beside compiles; against alone, compiles 0.995, benchmarks 0.969 - while the measured
core's clock went from about 2,000 to 4,600 MHz from block to block. The offset does not follow the clock: the fast end
is about 6% slower after the run in every condition. The fastest tenth re-measured: 1.066 (rule 3).

**The spread at generation 102** (session 61): fastest 0.1520 against seed 73's 0.1469 and 72's 0.1485; at 32 bits
0.1731 against 0.1896 and 0.1828; smallest 14,167 against 14,079 - by the end even at the 64-bit fast end (0.1402,
0.1399) and ahead at 32 bits.

**The remote checks of seed 73's front** (results/xcheck-seed74.md): Spearman 0.977, 0.974, 0.984 at dream's three
builds, 0.856 on the Tegra - the earlier check's 0.87-0.93 was the sample (results/drafts/xcheck-agreement-546.md).

**The rules**: 3 ATTENTION (1.066); 5 ATTENTION in name only - the 34 32-bit failures are cpt16's reach (517), which the
batch's fail32 patch names; 6 PASS (14,079); 7 PASS (rtiplus on 30%, working); 8 tail retired; 9 PASS (2 designs rely
on the overlap: no change).

**The failure**: tools/clean-runs.sh, run before every step of a chain, removes a run directory whose pack is in a
chain's packs/ (Iteration 541) - also while that chain runs. The seed step's directory went before the experiments, and
with it the tests', validation's and jail's logs that a later step copies; each experiment then failed at `cp
.../tests.log`. Their reports are complete in the failed packs. Fixed in the after-seed-74 batch.

Facts: held out (heldout.py), sieve's Spearman 0.904 (the fastest tenth 0.889), regret +0.0% - the selection's fastest
is the held-out fastest; appbench.py on 16 of the re-measured front (88), Spearman 0.900, every output right; progress
- the smallest gained in 30 generations (the last at 128 of 132), the best speed by 2% or more in 29 (the last at 123);
the pack's own checks PASS, 7 IDENTICAL, jail PASS; results-measured-here.patch 4 files, not applied.

---

# Evolved VM designs

30015 designs evaluated, 21507 alive, 3887 screened - not measured (Iteration 200). Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann, apps,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).


The Q-best design (binary + image x run time x memory - the knee; information): 9f1b82d15a, speed 0.379, binary + image 18,967, memory 136 KB - Q 0.297 of s6-cv8b's.

## The front's genomes

Each design above, and any other the front was measured again with, as one JSON line: its record without the raw timings. A tool that takes a database (--db) reads this report as one - the lines that are not JSON are skipped - so results/ is all a new chat needs to rebuild a front design.
